Palmer left out by England as Jobe Bellingham and King earn maiden call-ups

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Thomas Tuchel has left Cole Palmer out of his latest England squad, but Jobe Bellingham and Josh King have both received their first call-ups for the Nations League.

Palmer has made a strong start to the season for Chelsea, racking up eight goal involvements in all competitions (five goals, three assists).

However, having been left out of the World Cup squad, and then dropping out before the extended three-week break earlier this season, Tuchel said he "misses too many opportunities to play for England", and the 24-year-old has now been omitted.

He is one of two big-name omissions, with Phil Foden also not included despite a return to form for Manchester City.

Tuchel has instead looked to new faces, with Jobe Bellingham joining older brother Jude in the squad, having impressed for Borussia Dortmund so far this season.

He has played in all but one of Dortmund's matches in all competitions so far, and ranks second for tackles won (17), interceptions (19) and possession won (67), all behind Waldemar Anton.

Should Jobe Bellingham make his debut, the Bellinghams would become the fourth pair of brothers to represent England in the modern era.

King is the other player to receive his maiden call-up ahead of the final Nations League matches against Croatia and Spain.

For the final time in 2026… Your #ThreeLions squad for November camp!

The Fulham academy graduate was part of England's preparation camp ahead of the 2026 World Cup and has now been included in the squad proper after impressing at the start of the season with two goals and one assist in the Premier League.

There is also a return to the squad for Ben White for the first time since March, while Arsenal team-mate Declan Rice is back, having missed the last camp through injury.

The Three Lions still have to contend with injuries, with Bukayo Saka, Marcus Rashford, Alex Scott and Trevoh Chalobah all unable to be called upon, though Djed Spence is back in the squad for the first time since the World Cup after returning to fitness.

England will face Croatia at Wembley Stadium on November 12 before travelling to Madrid to face Spain three days later.

Tuchel's side currently sit second in Group A4, three points behind Spain, with the top two securing a place in the Nations League quarter-finals in March.

Leverkusen lacking quality in first Europa League loss

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Bayer Leverkusen sporting director Simon Rolfes believes the team were lacking quality in a game with few chances as they fell to a 1-0 defeat to Marseille in the Europa League.

Leverkusen suffered their first defeat in Europe this term, with Amine Gouiri's stunning 77th-minute strike securing all three points for Marseille.

It was a game with few clear-cut chances, though Leverkusen almost snatched a point through Patrik Schick late on, only for his header to be easily held by Jeffrey de Lange.

Leverkusen had won each of their first three Europa League games, meaning this defeat has not given their hopes of automatic qualification for the last 16 too big a dent at this stage.

However, the Werkself have now lost three of their last four matches and have suffered back-to-back defeats at home in all competitions, after a perfect start to the campaign at BayArena.

And Rolfes conceded that they struggled to hit their best level, though he remained positive about their position in the table.

"It was a game with few chances, and we weren't paying attention in one situation," he told RTL.

Our first defeat in the Europa League. #B04OM https://t.co/Es9zz6FF5c

— Bayer 04 Leverkusen (@bayer04_en) November 5, 2026

"We should have been better in terms of passing quality.

"We didn't play that well. Nevertheless, we have nine out of a possible twelve points in the Europa League."

Leverkusen accumulated just 0.9 expected goals (xG) from their 10 shots, with three of those on target, while Marseille had just eight attempts with two on Mark Flekken's goal.

This defeat ended a 10-game unbeaten streak at home in the Europa League (W7 D3), and they had scored 2.6 goals per game on average across that streak.

Thomas Tuchel explains Cole Palmer omission: ‘He misses too many opportunities’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The England manager has opted to ‘trust’ the players who impressed him in the last squad, while also offering first-time call-ups to Josh King and Jobe Bellingham

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Thomas Tuchel says he has “enough” No 10s in his England squad without selecting Cole Palmer, who has paid the price for pulling out of the September camp last time out and missing “too many opportunities to impress”.

The Chelsea star was a notable omission from Tuchel’s 25-man squad ahead of the Nations League games against Croatia and Spain and he was available for selection. Tuchel criticised Palmer for missing “too many camps with injuries” following his withdrawal from last month’s group and the manager has opted to stick with many of the same players who impressed him last time.

Tuchel has named several No 10s in his group, Morgan Rogers, Morgan Gibbs-White, Eberechi Eze, Jude Bellingham, as well as first-time call-ups Josh King and Jobe Bellingham, who both offer versatility in his midfield.

Palmer has not played for England since March and was missed the World Cup after struggling with injuries last season. He has played in all nine of Chelsea’s Premier League matches this season, but was also sent off in the defeat to Tottenham.

Speaking at a press conference at Wembley, Tuchel explained: “It counts when you are in camp and I just said it last time, he misses too many opportunities to impress. It was a long camp. It was two-and-a-half weeks and the players and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary throughout these weeks.

“That's why the credit goes to them. The trust is for them. If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress. So it's not personal. We will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country, but the competition is on and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.”

Tuchel added: “There are enough of No 10s, and we stick to the guys who were in camp because camp was excellent and we were happy with the last two-and-half weeks that we had together.

”The impact of all the players was immense throughout the whole camp and that's why we stick mainly with the group. We stick with all the offensive players and we have Josh King as a new player in there, but he can play four positions.”

Palmer was among five players to withdraw from Tuchel’s first England squad since the World Cup, along with Declan Rice, Marcus Rashford, Kobbie Mainoo and Tino Livramento. Only vice-captain Rice, who featured heavily at the World Cup, has since been recalled.

Tuchel is not expecting as many withdrawals this time and said he will continue to reward the players who turn up for England and take their opportunities to shine, while adding that he believes his message is getting through to his players.

“It's always about the ones who are in camp,” he said. “It's always the ones who don't withdraw and who walk the talk and come to camp and show that it matters and put the quality in. This is always what we will reward.

“That was always the message very, very early since we took charge, that it matters if you are in camp and it matters what you do in camp. And the last camp was an excellent one.”

It's Official: The PlayStation Blackout Protest Did Nothing

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Sony keeps growing despite the announced discontinuation of physical media

Disgruntled players tried to boycott Sony in August after the company announced it would discontinue physical media. However, it seems that the PlayStation Digital Blackout protest was a bust. Sony released official data for its second fiscal quarter, making it clear the boycott didn't move the needle. Instead, the company continued to grow, proving its point that losing physical media will not affect Sony as a business.

With Sony ceasing disc production in January 2028 and allegedly trying to establish a digital game monopoly, PlayStation owners who were unhappy with its recent business practices organized a boycott. From Aug. 23 until Aug. 30, participants pledged not to make in-game purchases, buy digital games, or renew PlayStation Plus subscriptions. However, the protest gained less traction than expected. Circana's video game industry advisor, Mat Piscatella, initially noted that there hadn't been any significant change in player count or engagement during the blackout.

The financial records have confirmed the protest's insignificance to Sony's business. Not only did sales and engagement not go down, but they actually went up, with Insomniac Games' Marvel's Wolverine leading the charge with 2.4 million units sold. Compared to the previous quarter, monthly active users increased by 2 million and digital sales rose by ¥138.8 billion — ¥158.7 billion if you count add-ons, which is about $996.2 million, using FYQ2's average conversion rate.

With the launch of Grand Theft Auto 6 on Nov. 19, numbers are only expected to keep rising this fiscal quarter. With the launch date fast approaching, many players will make sure they secure their pre-orders in order to be able to preload GTA 6 ahead of release. Plus, since the physical edition of GTA 6 doesn't contain a disc, most will simply stick to purchasing it on the PS Store, driving the number of digital sales even higher.

It may be a harsh reality for physical media advocates, but the truth is that Sony's decision to pursue an all-digital future has not had a negative impact so far. Given estimates that Microsoft's next-gen Xbox could cost up to $1,800 — and that's without knowing if it'll even include a disc drive — Sony will likely continue to prosper regardless of moving away from physical media.

Sony's new Japan-only PS5 will be hard to get, but may be worth the price

Why One Key Name is Missing From Parker’s Back Room Team | West Ham News

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

All change within West Ham’s coaching set up this week.

One name that has been mentioned by supporters since Scott Parker’s arrival, yet seems missing from the Hammers new – look post Nuno back room team is Mark Robson, with questions asked as to whether he would be involved within the new coaching set up as part of Parkers’s team.

While Robson has remained part of West Ham’s coaching structure, he actually returned to his role as the Academy of Football’s Senior Professional Development Phase Coach and Under-21s Lead Coach in the summer of 2026.

Robson did work with Nuno upon the Portuguese manager’s arrival from Nottingham Forest

According to the club, this was the same position he had previously held before stepping into first-team coaching roles. His move back to the Academy coincided with changes behind the scenes following Nuno’s decision to reshape his coaching staff in the summer, including the addition of Rui Pedro Silva, who departed earlier in the week alongside Nuno and Rui Barbosa.

When Scott Parker arrived, he too preferred to work with trusted personnel, bringing long-time colleague Jonathan Hill in as Assistant Head Coach. Yet Robson’s return to the Under-21s should never be viewed as a demotion.

The Upton Park-born coach is regarded as one of the most respected development coaches in English football. After a playing career that included helping West Ham secure promotion to the Premier League in 1992/93, Robson built an impressive coaching CV across both youth and senior football.

His biography confirms that he worked with Charlton Athletic’s academy and first team, held coaching roles at Gillingham, Peterborough United and Barnet, became an FA Youth Coach Educator, and later spent several years coaching within England’s youth international set-up. He also worked at Norwich City and Aston Villa before returning to West Ham in 2021.

Perhaps most significantly, much of that career has been centred on one thing, developing young players.

West Ham’s Academy has long prided itself on producing talent capable of making the jump into the first team. In that environment, Robson’s expertise is arguably more valuable than almost any role he could occupy elsewhere within the football club.

The situation is also not entirely new. Robson similarly returned to the Under-21s in 2024 after spending the 2023/24 campaign working within the first-team coaching set-up.

West Ham have repeatedly shown a willingness to move him between senior and academy football depending on where they feel his knowledge can have the greatest impact.

With suggestions that West Ham will continue to place significant importance on producing homegrown talent, keeping one of England’s most highly regarded youth coaches with the Under-21s may be just as important as any appointment made alongside Parker in the first-team dugout.

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.

I was suggesting Mark Robson as an interim manager till end of season, because he obviously gets on with this players and he has them playing quick, effective football that is good to watch. The 2nd half of WHU against Cardiff with the ball moved quickly forwards with sharp passes, reminded me of the U21 style of play.

Still think there is a need to look after ex-U21s who are around the 1st team because they still need one on one coaching, to concentrate on their weaknesses. Just a few hours a week, or individual work with a 1st team player who looks after them, or an advanced academy abroad.

I look at a player such as Fearon (he is not the only one) and I think you have the type of player who with 2-3 seasons on loan and a good club (Como with Fabregas – ain’t going to happen, but..) he would come back a very accomplished and well rounded player who would be a real asset to the club. Same for all the ex-academy lads already in the squad.

Depends on manager’s approach, coaches & Koppen, plus whoever looks after loans/ongoing ex-U21 development but important phase in their development.

Not a demotion for Mark Robson, absolutely. Working with youngsters might be what he prefers as well. A lot to do, to work on and hone. also rewarding.

Goodbye, Bradford Pear! This Native Tree Has Beautiful Blooms, Berries and Fall Color

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

6 Berry-Bearing Shrubs to Plant Now and Feed Hungry Birds Through Winter

Why plant an invasive ornamental tree when you could have one that's just as beautiful but brings so much more to the garden?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Look, I'm calling it: the Bradford pear has had its moment. For years, its blooms have been everywhere, but now its the turn of an even prettier native tree to take its spot – and it definitely helps that it's a triple threat when it comes to blossoms, berries, and beautiful fall foliage!

I know, I know; it's a big claim, especially as so many people rate the Bradford pear for its frothy white flowers, not to mention the pretty show its leaves put on when fall arrives. Still, there's a less attractive side to Bradford pears – and I'm not just talking about the unpleasant stench its flowers put out.

That's right, folks! As well as being notoriously unpleasant-smelling, the Bradford pear's branches are prone to splitting, and these trees can contribute to the spread of invasive Callery pears, too. So, if you're thinking about planting a new ornamental tree, it might be time to consider a more wildlife-friendly alternative.

My suggested swap for the invasive Bradford pear? Why, the serviceberry, of course!

This beautiful native tree pretty much does it all; think plenty of springtime charm in the form of beautiful blossoms, followed by delicious berries, and wrapped up nicely with some gorgeous fall foliage. And unlike Bradford pear, it's a valuable addition to a garden that welcomes local wildlife.

Serviceberry, also known as juneberry or shadbush, belongs to the Amelanchier genus. Depending on the species and variety, it can grow as a large shrub or a small tree, making it a useful option for gardens where a full-sized shade tree would be too much.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

It is one of those plants that gives back much more than it takes, providing an early source of nectar and pollen for pollinators, sweet edible berries that work brilliantly in pies, jams and other homemade treats (although birds love them, too), and gorgeous fall foliage that promises to paint your yard in shades of yellow, orange and red before winter arrives.

All that from one relatively compact native tree? You'd better believe it!

One of the things I love most about serviceberry (as if all of the above weren't reason enough to be a fan!) is that most species grow well in full sun or partial shade, making it more accessible to people with all sorts of gardens. Just be sure to pop it in a spot with well-draining soil, as they won't appreciate sitting in waterlogged ground.

For many homeowners, this native tree is a no-brainer, because what do you mean it offers the spring flowers that made Bradford pear so popular, along with edible fruit, beautiful fall color and valuable food for wildlife? Tick, tick, tick, and tick!

While it won't necessarily grow as quickly or have the same uniform, upright shape as a Bradford pear, I think that's part of its appeal. And, with the world changing as quickly as it is, I think we can all agree that a garden tree should do more than look pretty for a few weeks each year.

A native beauty that gives back to local critters and puts out fruit that taste every bit as good as blueberries, then? Sign me up!

Kayleigh is an enthusiastic (sometimes too enthusiastic!) gardener and has worked in media for over a decade. She previously served as digital editor at Stylist magazine, and has written extensively for Ideal Home, Woman & Home, Homes & Gardens, and a handful of other titles. Kayleigh is passionate about wildlife-friendly gardening, and recently cancelled her weekend plans to build a mini pond when her toddler found a frog living in their water barrel. As such, her garden – designed around the stunning magnolia tree at its centre – is filled to the brim with pollinator-friendly blooms, homemade bird feeders, and old logs for insects to nest in.

Scientists removed the glare from a James Webb telescope image — and unmasked a hidden planet

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Using machine learning, astronomers developed a new way of looking at James Webb telescope data that allows far-off planets to emerge from their star's glare.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

When it comes to spotting planets orbiting distant stars, the biggest hurdle is often separating the signal from the noise — specifically, trying to distinguish a planet from the stellar glare around it. That's true even for the most sophisticated telescopes, including the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST).

Now, astronomers have developed a new way to spot planets that were formerly drowned out in a sea of light. Using a combination of modeling and machine learning techniques, they picked out a previously hidden giant exoplanet in data from JWST. The results were published Nov. 4 in the journal Nature.

"Planets are a million times fainter than their stars," study first author Rodrigo Ferrer-Chavez, an astronomer at Northwestern University, said in a statement. "So, to image exoplanets, we need to remove that glare."

Telescopes like JWST use instruments called coronagraphs to block out some of this excess light, similar to the way the moon and a pinhole camera operate during a total solar eclipse. But even the best coronagraphs have limitations — for example, they can't block all the glare, and the telescopes themselves move.

Even the most stable telescopes vibrate almost imperceptibly as they collect light, so the images a telescope produces contain ever-so-slight mismatches compared with what the instrument actually sees. These slight shifts can be enough to obscure even very large exoplanets.

To address this, Ferrer-Chavez and his team developed a "digital twin" of JWST that virtually recreates how light travels through the telescope's detectors, minus any movement-based imperfections. To fine-tune this digital replica, the researchers used computational models based in real-world physics alongside machine learning techniques similar to those used to train AI. But the authors stressed that this is not an AI model.

"Because we already know the physics, we don't need AI to understand what's happening," study co-author Jason Wang, also an astrophysicist at Northwestern, said in a statement.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

The researchers named their approach Physics-based Learning for Imaging Planets, or FLIP.

Using FLIP, the team analyzed data from three stars, including 51 Eridani, which is located around 97 light-years from Earth and was already suspected to have a giant exoplanet orbiting it. Although the planet, called 51 Eridani b, had been detected by ground-based telescopes, it was drowned out by light in previous JWST observations. With FLIP, however, 51 Eridani b popped out from the dataset.

The scientists hope this approach will help astronomers pick out even more hard-to-spot exoplanets, especially ones that look like the planets in our own backyard. In the nearly 35-year history of exoplanet hunting, the vast majority of planets we've found have been radically different from those in our own solar system, partially because larger and more reflective worlds are easier to spot with current telescopes.

"It's not that [solar-system-like planets] don't exist," Ferrer-Chavez said. "It's that we can't see them."

Ferrer-Chavez, R., Wang, J. J., Bouman, K. L., Freeman, W. T., Pueyo, L., Feng, B. Y., & Perrin, M. D. (2026). Imaging exoplanets below the telescope stability limit. Nature, 659(8139), 58–62. https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-026-11213-z

Joanna Thompson is a science journalist and runner based in New York. She holds a B.S. in Zoology and a B.A. in Creative Writing from North Carolina State University, as well as a Master's in Science Journalism from NYU's Science, Health and Environmental Reporting Program. Find more of her work in Scientific American, The Daily Beast, Atlas Obscura or Audubon Magazine.

Serie A: Spalletti’s Juventus breathes and pushes Allegri towards his end in Naples

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Juventus won a shock victory against Naples (2-0) this Sunday evening, during the 10th day of Serie A. After a lively first period, but without a goal, the Bianconeri ended up making the difference after returning from the locker room. Kerim-Sam Alajbegovic broke the deadlock in the 70th minute with a superb left-footed shot from distance, unstoppable for Meret. A first goal which freed Luciano Spalletti’s men, before Jérémie Boga came to make the break in the 87th minute.

Having struggled in recent weeks, the two teams approached this meeting with the ambition of relaunching their season. Napoli, however, had several opportunities in the first half, but came up against a great Vicario, while Cabal found the post for Juventus. The Neapolitans’ task was further complicated with the expulsion of Marianucci in the 75th minute, after a second warning. This success allows the Old Lady to regain color in a major shock, while Naples leaves Turin with new disillusionment.

Zcash (ZEC) OI Down 7.8% as NU7 Upgrade Goes Live

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Zcash open interest has declined 7.8%, according to the data from analytical platform Coinglass.

Open interest (OI) refers to the value of futures contracts that are still open. In other words, the rise in OI means more active positions.

When a price and OI decline simultaneously, it usually suggests deleveraging. The traders might be closing positions or their positions might be liquidated.

ZEC is trading near $1,286, down 1.5% over the past 24 hours. The price’s pullback comes amid a broader market correction, with BTC down 5.75% in 24 hours.

Zcash gained 7.69% on November 4, rising on high expectations surrounding NU7. The upgrade introduces several changes to the network's transaction processing and reward structure.

As the upgrade went live on November 5, the target block interval went from 75 seconds to 25 seconds. According to the project’s statement, the change makes private payments confirm three times faster.

In addition to that, NU7 introduces a new transaction fee allocation model. 60% of the fees will go directly to a reserve for future network reserves, while the miners receive the remaining 40%.

Other changes include increased capacity for the Orchard shielded pool and new limits for wallet protection from spam.

So far, the market response has been mixed amid Friday’s correction. However, the ZEC chart is currently showing signs of an uptrend.

The price is testing support around $1,250, a level that could influence its next move. A sustained recovery above the $1,260-1,275 corridor would stabilize the further rebound.

On the other hand, a break below $1,230 would mean a failed recovery and a prolonged downtrend.

Sydney's Powerhouse Parramatta revealed ahead of public opening tomorrow

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Photography by Rory Gardiner, Powerhouse Studio, Christina Yao

The Powerhouse Parramatta museum in Sydney by architecture studios Moreau Kusunoki and Genton, which is described as the largest cultural project in Australia since the Sydney Opera House, is set to open tomorrow.

Located on the banks of the Parramatta River in western Sydney, the distinctive Powerhouse Parramatta museum is wrapped by a lattice exoskeleton of steel that acts both as facade and structure. It was designed to “pioneer a new museum model” that redefines contemporary museum practice and culture.

“We designed the new Powerhouse Parramatta with the mission of calling the museum paradigm into question and conceiving a platform with limitless potential that evolves over time and through its users,” said Moreau Kusunoki.

Paris-based architecture studio Moreau Kusunoki, in collaboration with Australian architecture firm Genton, split the 30,000-square-metre museum into two blocky forms connected on the upper levels.

A system of three-layered steel lattice wraps the exterior of the building, referencing the iconic structure of Sydney Harbour Bridge while also connecting to the industrial heritage and manufacturing traditions of the site.

“Together with engineer Jun Sato, we developed the concept of ‘lattice’ for the structural exoskeleton – a system of intricate lattices that are wrapped around the spaces of the museum and support their dynamic programming, providing total interior freedom,” Moreau Kusunoki explained.

“Through the dissolution of the structure into a fine, efficient three-layered lattice, we conceived an envelope that transcends scale in order to exist simultaneously as both intimate and iconic, becoming the museum’s most recognisable design element.”

The lattice exoskeleton serves as the building’s facade as well as structure, which enables column-free interior exhibition spaces that support the museum’s diverse programming.

These include Exhibition Space 1, which is described as the largest column-free exhibition space in Australia. At 18 metres high and spanning over 2,000 square metres, it features a mechanical hoisting door in five sections, allowing it to open to the public riverside terrace.

Along with seven more traditional exhibition spaces, the institution contains 30 residential studios, numerous co-working spaces and other community-led learning facilities.

Beneath the lattice exoskeleton, much of the facade is fully glazed to filter natural light into the interiors. These interstitial spaces are informed by the Japanese concept of mâ – the interval between things, people, and time – shaping moments of pause that foster reflection and informal interaction.

“The concept of ‘in-between’, or mâ in Japanese, is central to the design approach in all our projects,” said Moreau Kusunoki. “We carved these spaces throughout the museum’s design as an indispensable factor of placemaking.”

“In contrast with the opaque nature of the presentation spaces, we modelled the mâ spaces as transparent and full of natural light, looking over the Parramatta River below and onto the horizon and country beyond,” it continued.

“The potential of the interstitial spaces gives way for life and meaning to evolve and to naturally appear in between the defined spaces, while events and interactions arise ‘through the cracks’, thereby organically enhancing the impact of the project.”

The two blocks, which were designed to open up on the ground floor, stand on two sides of a riverside terrace that sits above a garden that slopes down to the river. According to the museum, two-thirds of the site is dedicated to landscaped public space, with indigenous plant species planted under the guidance of the local First Nation community.

A rooftop terrace features a garden and a greenhouse, while an observatory with telescopes sits beneath a retractable roof. The museum’s ING Garden Pavilion will be used for hosting talks and workshops alongside community and industry programmes.

The neutral palette continues from the exterior to the interiors, with internal walls clad with glassfibre-reinforced concrete (GRC) panels of various textures.

They were made with quartz aggregate, waterglass-finish concrete and hand-applied oystershell render, adding tactile surfaces to the interior to create a calm exhibition viewing experience.

The building has been designed to operate at net-zero carbon.

Sustainability is embedded across the project, from renewable energy and water systems to zero-waste exhibition principles, making it the first public building in Australia to be assessed as a 6 Star Designed project under the Green Building Council of Australia‘s new Green Star Buildings assessment tool.

Five major international exhibitions will open tomorrow as part of the opening programmes of Powerhouse Parramatta, including Task Eternal, an immersive exhibition exploring humanity’s journey into space, which will be presented at Exhibition Space 1, and designed by Chinese architecture studio Open Architecture.

Dezeen is partnering with Powerhouse Parramatta to host two talks exploring the design and making of the landmark new museum. as part of the opening celebration.

The first brings together the architects, engineers and construction teams behind the building, while the second sees Dezeen co-CEO Benedict Hobson in conversation with OMA/AMO managing partner David Gianotten about The Mall – an ambitious exhibition examining the evolution of shopping centres and their influence on architecture, retail and contemporary culture.

A total of 179 events will take place across three weeks following the opening day as part of the opening festival programme, showcasing over 3,000 objects, including over 1,600 from Powerhouse Museum’s own collection.

Dezeen will also publish an exclusive video interview with architects Moreau Kusunoki and Genton, alongside four social videos exploring the museum’s inaugural exhibitions, including Task Eternal, The Mall and Es Devlin’s The Turning, showcasing the breadth of design, creativity and ideas behind the museum’s opening programme.

The creation of the new Powerhouse Parramatta museum, and the expanded research and public facilities at Powerhouse Castle Hill completed in 2024, form the $1.3 billion infrastructure renewal programme for the largest museum group in Australia. The renewal of the heritage Powerhouse Ultimo is still on going and expected to complete by late 2028.

Other recent landmark projects completed in Sydney include the Western Sydney International Airport, open to public last month, and Sydney Fish Market, which claims to be the largest fish market in the southern hemisphere.

Photography by Rory Gardiner, Powerhouse Studio, Christina Yao

Ce vendredi 6 novembre à 18h00, Zinédine Zidane annoncera sa deuxième liste à la tête de l'équipe de France depuis le siège de la FFF. Confronté à une avalanche de blessures, le sélectionneur tricolore s'apprête à repenser son secteur défensif... avec le retour de Lucas Hernandez ?

Pour aborder ce rassemblement capital en Ligue des nations, le staff technique des Bleus doit composer avec une cascade de forfaits majeurs dans ses lignes arrières. En effet, William Saliba n'est pas disponible pour cette échéance internationale, le défenseur central étant tout juste revenu d'une blessure au dos. Un coup dur auquel s'ajoute l'absence prolongée de Maxence Lacroix. Forfait en raison d'une déchirure aux ischio-jambiers, le joueur pourrait même être absent jusqu'à la fin de l'année. Enfin, Jules Koundé est également annoncé indisponible pour ces rendez-vous de novembre, n'étant pas encore remis de son entorse de la cheville gauche. Un casse-tête défensif.

Face à ces défections d'envergure, Zinédine Zidane explorerait plusieurs solutions d'expérience et de polyvalence pour rebâtir son arrière-garde. D'après L'Équipe, Lucas Hernandez et Castello Lukeba feraient ainsi partie des joueurs pressentis pour créer la surprise dans le secteur défensif de cette deuxième liste. Le joueur du Paris Saint-Germain sort d'une période positive avec la formation parisienne et offre une solution très appréciable dans l'axe ou à gauche. De son côté, Castello Lukeba possède un registre similaire. Le pensionnaire du RB Leipzig présente également l'avantage tactique de pouvoir évoluer dans l'axe central ainsi qu'au poste de latéral gauche. Une opportunité idéale pour les Bleus.

Au-delà de ces deux recours polyvalents, un autre profil inattendu pourrait créer l'événement dans le couloir gauche de la défense tricolore. Selon Foot Mercato, le latéral gauche Dayann Méthalie, qui évolue actuellement à Sunderland, a également reçu une pré-convocation de la part du staff des Bleus. Reste à savoir si le sélectionneur validera définitivement cette option ce vendredi à 18h00. Cette équipe de France renouvelée sera rapidement mise à l'épreuve lors des deux derniers matches de phase de poules de Ligue des nations. Les hommes de Zidane défieront l'Italie le 12 novembre à Milan à 20h45, avant de retrouver la Turquie le 15 novembre à Bordeaux à 20h45.

World of Warcraft: Forever's launch has been downright impressive—MMOs are notoriously difficult to run servers for, particularly when it comes to launch, where players will get bottlenecked into servers, throwing devs into a catch 22: Do they increase capacity and wind up wasting money in the long run while things die down, or do they ride out the wave? And yet, after the chaos of the beta, it's been buttery smooth.

Which is why I'm willing to forgive Blizzard for a weird quirk of its realmless layering technology—cities have been empty, empty as hell. Which is a shame, given I know that hundreds of thousands of players are technically on this thing—and yet, none of them are in my Stormwind.

Well, Forever's devs are working on a fix, per the official forums: "Just wanted to chime in," says Aggrend, "And say that we are aware of some issues with certain areas not feeling as populated as they should. We will be tweaking some numbers tomorrow to help things feel a bit more lively. Thanks for the feedback on this one!"

That's a relief to hear, especially given I'm on a roleplay server, and there have been problems with this sort of thing where phasing has interrupted the flow of narrative commerce in the past. Cities, especially, should feel lived-in, and it's hard to create a sense of community when people you could be writing with keep bamfing in and out of existence.

But even if you aren't a roleplayer like yours truly, I think you should be gladdened by this news—big cities are one of the places where crowding isn't a problem at all, but rather, the thing you want. They're social hubs, and rubbing shoulder to shoulder with the masses at the auction house is kind of the whole point.

At least anecdotally, my Stormwind's been looking a lot more populated, and I'm sure that as populations settle to sane levels and Blizzard keeps chipping away at this tech, the streets'll be bustling again.

Marco Palestra has admitted he still has plenty of work to do after a difficult start to life at Chelsea.

The 21-year-old arrived from Atalanta in the summer with a strong reputation and became the first signing of Xabi Alonso’s reign, but injuries delayed his competitive debut.

He finally started in Chelsea’s 2-0 win over Manchester United last weekend, although his performance drew some criticism for his defensive positioning.

Even so, Palestra remains highly regarded at Stamford Bridge and appears determined to prove why Chelsea invested heavily in him.

According to the Evening Standard, Palestra openly acknowledged that he needs to improve if he wants to become a success at Chelsea.

Reflecting on the moment he learned the move was happening, the Italian said: “I was just happy because, when I was young, I never thought I could be a Chelsea player.”

He added: “Now I am still happy, but I need to improve myself to win with this team.”

Chelsea paid around £45million to sign Palestra from Atalanta, while the defender agreed a six-year contract with an option for another season.

There was significant competition for his signature before Chelsea moved quickly to secure the deal, and CaughtOffside reported that Liverpool and Tottenham had also shown interest before he chose Stamford Bridge.

Palestra’s first few months have not gone according to plan, but that does not mean Chelsea should be concerned.

His early-season injury disrupted the adaptation process, and Alonso himself admitted in September that Palestra had been frustrated after impressing during pre-season. The Chelsea boss also stressed that the youngster would get opportunities once fully fit.

The more important point is that Palestra has qualities Chelsea clearly value. He can play as a right-back or wing-back, can also cover on the left and offers the kind of tactical flexibility Alonso likes.

A CaughtOffside report from July also highlighted Alonso’s admiration for his versatility, physical strength and mentality.

For now, patience is the sensible approach.

Palestra has barely had a chance to build rhythm, and judging a 21-year-old after one difficult Premier League start would be premature. Chelsea signed him for his long-term potential, not instant perfection.

If he stays fit and adapts to the intensity of English football, the qualities that made him one of Serie A’s most exciting young defenders should eventually become far more obvious at Stamford Bridge.

Binance hackers had 8 weeks of access before $6.3B exploit, Security Alliance estimates

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The cybersecurity company flagged similar multi-stage intrusion patterns behind other high-profile crypto heists, including the $388 million Bitget hack.

Non-profit cybersecurity organization Security Alliance (SEAL) estimates that the North Korea-linked TraderTraitor gained access to Binance’s systems about eight weeks before the $6.3 billion exploit on Oct. 29.

The hacking group gained access to Binance’s systems sometime in September, wrote SEAL in a Thursday report, adding that threat actors can navigate these systems with a “high degree of stealth over prolonged periods of time” after initial intrusion.

SEAL identified at least four “front companies” associated with TraderTraitor’s fake recruitment campaigns in 2026, including citalab.net, citalab.com, bitfit.io and oddium.io.

The cybersecurity organization’s analysis pointed to similar multi-stage intrusion patterns in other high-profile crypto hacks. It estimated that attackers had about six weeks of access before the $388 million Bitget hack, six weeks of access to LayerZero before the vulnerability that led to the $293 million Kelp DAO exploit and about three weeks before conducting the $24 million AFX exploit.

Cointelegraph contacted Binance for comment on the estimates but did not receive a response by publication. Binance attributed the $6.3 billion hack to a fake job interview that enabled hackers to compromise an employee device and steal credentials to breach the exchange’s wallet infrastructure.

Binance traced the attack campaign back to June, when a separate employee’s device was compromised. The exchange detected the incident and replaced the device, but said that attackers had already gathered information about its team and wallet infrastructure, according to a Tuesday X post.

The US Federal Bureau of Investigation attributed the attack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor, in a Saturday report. Kim Yo Jong, sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, dismissed the accusations.

Paraguay Bank-Freeze Puts XRP Custody Risk in Focus

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Home » Trendsetter News » Paraguay Bank-Freeze Puts XRP Custody Risk in Focus

A mass bank-account freeze in Paraguay have renewed discussion of XRP and self-custody.

More than 3 million people locked out of their bank accounts overnight: that is the claim driving a video by Edo Farina, a popular researcher covering banks and crypto. He alleges that Paraguay’s central bank took control of digital bank Ueno, using the purported disruption to argue for XRP and self-custody.

Edo Farina’s central comparison is between allegedly inaccessible bank deposits and the XRP Ledger continuing to process transactions. “The XRP Ledger kept settling transactions every few seconds like any other day,” he says.

That distinction concerns network availability, not investment performance. In the same video, he puts XRP near $1.25, down roughly 5%, and bitcoin below $77,000, also falling nearly 5%. These are figures quoted during the recording, not current market prices.

He identifies $1.60 as XRP’s key resistance level and says he would not call a reversal before it is reclaimed.

His broader argument that banking failures will increase demand for XRP as a settlement asset is a forecast; continued ledger operation alone does not demonstrate increased adoption or demand.

The YouTube video’s practical emphasis is on spreading exposure across banks, keeping emergency cash and holding cryptocurrency keys outside exchanges. Exchange-held assets can become inaccessible through platform restrictions, insolvency proceedings or legal action.

But Edo overstates the protection when he says nobody can seize XRP held with personal keys. Native XRP has no issuer clawback mechanism; the ledger does support clawback for certain issued tokens. Neither distinction makes an owner immune to legal orders, stolen keys or compelled transfers.

His claim that physical property cannot be confiscated is similarly too broad. Gold and real estate remain subject to seizure under applicable law.

Holding keys removes reliance on an exchange to authorize transfers, but it does not protect XRP’s market value or make an unverified banking story evidence of its future demand.

Arsenal have been warned not to make a rushed decision over Viktor Gyokeres when the January transfer window opens.

The Sweden international has endured a difficult season, with Kai Havertz currently ahead of him in Mikel Arteta’s preferred starting XI. That has inevitably fuelled speculation over a possible winter exit, especially with some pundits questioning whether Gyokeres is the right fit for Arsenal’s system.

However, former Gunners striker Jeremie Aliadiere believes selling him midway through the season could create an even bigger problem.

Speaking to Metro, Aliadiere argued that Arsenal must think carefully before sanctioning Gyokeres’ departure.

“I think it’s all depending on the finances how much they can get for him and who to replace him with,” he said.

His main concern is squad depth. Havertz may be first choice, but Arsenal would leave themselves dangerously short if Gyokeres departed without a replacement already secured.

“If Havertz gets injured, which obviously we’ve seen in the past – he’s had a few injuries and he can pick up injuries – you just end up with no striker at all,” Aliadiere added.

That warning comes amid growing debate over Gyokeres’ future. CaughtOffside reported in October that Micah Richards believes Arsenal should consider moving him on if his form does not improve.

There is a fair argument that Gyokeres has not fully adapted to Arteta’s preferred style. Sky Sports recently highlighted how Havertz offers more in possession, combination play and pressing, which helps explain why he has become the favoured option.

But that does not automatically mean Arsenal should sell.

Gyokeres still offers something different. His power, direct running and ability to attack space can be useful in games where Arsenal need a more traditional No.9. The striker has grown frustrated with his limited Premier League starts and could consider leaving.

From Arsenal’s perspective, the smartest approach would be to keep him unless a clear upgrade is ready to arrive.

Selling first and searching for a replacement later would be an unnecessary gamble. Arsenal are competing on multiple fronts, and one injury to Havertz could quickly make Gyokeres far more important than he currently appears.

Bitcoin holds $75K as AI regulation fears see KOSPI halted after 8% crash

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin consolidated as fears over US AI regulation spread to Asia, causing South Korea to announce measures to calm stock-market volatility.

Bitcoin (BTC) held above $75,000 on Friday as a sell-off in US technology stocks spread to Asian markets.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD trading near its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), at $75,733.

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50-week SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

The pair fell as much as 6.6% on Thursday as US stock markets saw sustained selling throughout the Wall Street trading session. The S&P 500 and tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite closed down 4.4% and 5.5%, respectively.

Nasdaq Composite Index one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

On Friday, Asian stocks extended the sell-off. South Korea’s KOSPI Composite Index fell more than 8% shortly after the open, triggering a circuit breaker that halted trading for 20 minutes. The index then pared losses to close 6% lower at 5,059.69 points, its lowest close since March 31.

KOSPI one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

At an emergency meeting, Korea’s deputy prime minister and finance minister Lee Hyoung-il announced measures to reduce forced selling.

Brokerages will be exempt from a requirement to maintain a 140% collateral ratio on margin loans for three months from Monday, local media reported. Authorities said they would prepare futher measures if volatility increased.

“We recognize with gravity the expanding volatility in global stock markets following the U.S. midterm elections,” Chosun quoted an unnamed government source as saying.

Analysts linked Thursday’s Wall Street losses to fears of tighter AI regulation after Democrats won the House and Senate. Those fears compounded existing market concerns about the sector’s capital spending.

Related: Bitcoin, Ether ETFs post $530M outflows as election gains evaporate

Citing an X post by Democratic Rep. Yassamin Ansari, who called for a “pause on advanced AI development,” market commentary service The Kobeissi Letter said an outright US ban on AI was unlikely.

“In our view, a ban of AI will not happen in the US (with near-certainty). Such a ban, if even possible to enforce, would mean the US surrendering its position in the global AI race to China. That won’t happen,” it wrote in an X post.

Kobeissi noted that Thursday’s losses were concentrated in tech, with the S&P 500 declining just about 2.5% excluding the sector. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index, which tracks 30 semiconductor companies, finished Thursday 12.2% lower.

Philadelphia Semiconductor Index one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Economist Mohamed El-Erian also called for common ground on AI policyt.said that lawmakers would need to find common ground on AI policy.

“America’s ‘let it rip’ approach to AI aligns with the technology’s productivity potential and national security imperatives. However, it has fallen short not only on safety and diffusion, but also on bringing society along,” he wrote in a Substack note.

El-Erian said the backlash reflected “genuine concerns,” information failures and behavioral factors.

Cointelegraph previously reported that Democrat midterm sweeps under Republican presidents have historically been followed by strong stock returns. Bitcoin’s correlation with the S&P 500, has become more positive in recent months.

Huddersfield Town are top of the table after 14 fixtures and have lost only once in the league this season. HTAFC are three points clear of Stockport County and Oxford United, with Luton Town, Plymouth Argyle and Reading then chasing them down.

The Terriers drew 1-1 at home to Luton Town last time out with in-form attacker Joe Taylor getting their goal against the Hatters. They are back in action this weekend against Exeter City in the first round of the FA Cup.

Tozer, who played for Newport County, Cheltenham Town and Wrexham, has delivered his verdict on the third tier promotion picture on The Essential EFL Podcast: “Take nothing away from Stockport, Luton, Oxford, Plymouth Argyle. Drury, 40, joined the Terriers last January and worked under Liam Manning before taking over as caretaker in March. He has also worked as a coach in the past at Manchester United, West Brom, Valencia and Brentford so has an impressive CV behind him.

Drury has also managed to get the best out of Taylor following his return from his loan at Wigan Athletic. He has found the net on 10 occasions in the league already.

The Terriers need to hope both Fernandes and Taylor can stay fit as they aim to keep their momentum going. Their upcoming cup tie against Exeter City could be an opportunity to play some fringe players and youngsters.

How to Choose the Best Crypto Advertising Network

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Do this: ask an advertising provider for a sample campaign report before you ask for a proposal.

Can you see which audiences converted? Does conversion mean a registration, a wallet connection, or a completed transaction? Can you detect where the ads appeared and spending on placements that aren’t generating results?

These questions will tell you much more than a professionally made slide showing millions of impressions. After all, you are choosing a company to spend your budget, and you need to understand what it intends to buy, how it measures it, and what it changes.

Here is what you should be checking before you commit.

When a provider says it reaches “crypto users,” that’s not enough. Ask what qualifies someone for this label. Is it someone who once read a Bitcoin article, or an active trader who is comparing exchanges right now? Each of these suggests a very different audience.

Here is what a good answer sounds like. Ask Blockchain-Ads, the platform Coinbase, Binance and OKX buy on, and you get a segment with a definition and a recency window: active crypto traders, people who recently compared exchanges or wallets, or your own depositors as a retargeting group. That precision is what a demand-side platform is for, and Blockchain-Ads was the first one built for crypto, iGaming and finance. Hold every network on your shortlist to the same standard.

For example, if you are promoting a lending protocol, your question should be whether you can reach a segment of people interested in DeFi lending, and how recently they showed that interest. Then you should request an audience estimate for your target countries.

When you compare crypto advertising networks, a comparison of crypto ad networks gives you a starting shortlist. Then get specific:

A publisher list will tell you where your ads might appear. It won’t necessarily tell you who will see them.

There are two different buying approaches to understand here:

Let’s say you sell a wallet for active DeFi users. A crypto news placement could reach experienced users alongside casual readers. Audience targeting could reach relevant users while they browse other subjects.

Now here is the trick. Neither approach automatically wins. Your task here is to ask the provider what they support and whether you can apply contextual restrictions when you need them.

The most important test for an advertising network that serves crypto exchanges is whether it can connect the advertising spend to funded accounts and trading activity.

For a wallet, the event could be a completed swap.

For a blockchain game, it could be a player returning after a first session.

Give the provider your event definition and ask the following:

The best crypto advertising network for your project needs to measure the actual outcome.

Tip: A public transaction doesn’t automatically reveal which ad acquired the user. Blockchain-Ads’ attribution guide explains that campaign identifiers must be linked to account or wallet activity.

Start with the conversion definition. Then, examine the headline number.

Ask for evidence that matches your situation: similar product, target market, budget, and conversion event. Then check the following:

Minimum deposits, daily budgets, and service eligibility are separate commitments when you pick a crypto advertising network.

Blockchain-Ads, for example, publishes its minimum deposit and daily budget, and offers a self-serve platform or a managed option run by industry experts.

Ask any provider for the same clarity. You need to be able to confirm management fees, creative costs, top-up requirements, and the treatment of unused funds.

Finally, ask what the proposed budget can reasonably test. You need a clear explanation of how the funds will be split across countries, formats, and audiences.

A total CPA can hide expensive placements behind cheaper ones.

Ask for a demonstration of reporting by audience, creative, country, device, and placement. The idea here is to confirm which breakdowns are available together and which require support or an export request.

Try a very practical question during the demo:

“If mobile conversions drop next week, what can I do to investigate this?”

Ideally, you should be able to examine whether the delivery shifted toward a different audience or country.

Tip: Ask how quickly the reports update, and check whether existing customers can be excluded from acquisition campaigns. Otherwise, a campaign may receive credit for people you already acquired. Remember: the best crypto ads in a presentation are less useful than reporting that helps you decide what deserves your money.

Fraud protection needs much more explanation than a badge on a well-designed website.

Ask what gets filtered before buying, what gets checked afterward, and how you can report suspicious activity. Find out whether you can pause delivery during an investigation and what evidence the provider needs.

Blockchain-Ads, for instance, documents independent impression verification, traffic monitoring, allow and block lists, and frequency controls. It also asks advertisers reporting problems to supply the campaign ID and period.

Use these categories when you talk to providers:

Tip: Clarify the privacy requirements, too: what customer data you can upload, how it is protected, how deletion requests work, etc.

Finally, ask who will review performance and what would trigger a change.

Marko Rendevski, SVP Advertising Services & Partnerships at Blockchain-Ads, describes the principle clearly: “We don’t make decisions based only on CTR or a small number of clicks.”

Have the provider explain how they will assess your campaign against your target conversion cost and normal conversion delay. Someone who deposits several days after registration needs time to appear in the results.

If you are wondering how to choose a crypto ad network, finish it all with this one question:

“What will we know after this first test?”

Choosing a crypto advertising network is not simple. Before you commit, ask the provider to show you how it would build your audience, track your conversions, and report where your money went. Get the costs and the test plan in writing. You should leave that conversation only once you know what the first campaign will test and how you can judge the results.

FBI agent drains crypto wallets tied to 35 investigations, steals $2.47 million and plans Portugal escape

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The former counterintelligence agent used sensitive wallet credentials to execute roughly 100 fraudulent transfers.

A former FBI agent stole $2.47 million in crypto using classified investigative records, compromising federal investigations into criminal organizations and foreign adversaries.

The US Department of Justice disclosed on Nov. 5 that Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, pleaded guilty to wire fraud and money laundering after exploiting his access to crypto wallet credentials collected during FBI investigations.

His scheme involved about 100 unauthorized transfers using credentials tied to 35 separate FBI investigations, including wallets belonging to suspected criminals, adversarial nation-state actors, and at least one wallet the bureau created for an operation.

Prosecutors said the thefts compromised multiple investigations involving criminal groups and foreign adversaries, along with sensitive intelligence-gathering methods.

The disclosure exposes a significant internal security failure at the FBI, where an agent entrusted with protecting national security turned information gathered through federal investigations into personal enrichment.

According to prosecutors, Yaroch worked in the FBI's Boston Division from 2017 until early 2025, investigating threats involving a foreign adversary.

He subsequently joined the bureau's Counterintelligence and Espionage Division, where he had access to systems containing cryptocurrency credentials obtained through investigations.

The thefts began no later than December 2024, before his transfer to the division.

An FBI affidavit filed in August revealed that Yaroch searched bureau records for crypto wallets associated with foreign adversaries, memorized their recovery credentials, and transferred funds into a personal wallet.

Two routes intersected: access to wallet secrets in FBI records enabled transfers from investigation-linked wallets.

Investigative access to wallet credentials enabled personal transfers, including from at least one FBI operational wallet.

The affidavit's personal-wallet account predates the broader plea scope. The total proceeds and transfer count describe the entire scheme, not a single wallet balance. The diagram does not identify affected cases, all destinations, security controls or a network breach.

Schematic maps the credential-access account in the Aug. 1 affidavit onto the broader wallet categories and approximate scheme totals in DOJ's Nov. 5 plea announcement. Connector sizes are not quantitative. $2.47M is the rounded reported proceeds total; no new arithmetic.

The affidavit's personal-wallet account predates the broader plea scope. The total proceeds and transfer count describe the entire scheme, not a single wallet balance. The diagram does not identify affected cases, all destinations, security controls or a network breach.

He initially told investigators that frustration with the FBI's inability to disrupt certain adversarial crypto accounts had led him to take matters into his own hands.

However, the misconduct expanded into a broader theft and money laundering scheme.

After taking the crypto, Yaroch converted portions into other digital assets and moved funds through additional transactions to conceal their origins.

Between November 2025 and January 2026, he transferred approximately $93,082 into an account and used money moved between his accounts to cover credit card bills, vehicle payments, and other debts.

The Justice Department said his actions interfered with the FBI's ability to protect public safety and national security while compromising sources and methods used in criminal investigations and foreign intelligence collection.

Prosecutors did not identify the affected countries or investigations, leaving the full extent of the intelligence damage undisclosed.

Beyond paying personal debts, Yaroch was preparing to use the proceeds to establish a new life in Europe, according to federal investigators.

Court documents revealed that the former agent turned to ChatGPT for advice on investing approximately $1 million and relocating to the European Union.

After discussing his interest in vineyard ownership and a slower lifestyle, the AI chatbot recommended Portugal as a potential destination.

Yaroch subsequently took concrete steps toward establishing himself in the country.

On June 15, he granted power of attorney to Portuguese lawyers to register him with the country's tax authorities and obtain a tax identification number.

Investigators also discovered plans for a September trip to Portugal. The August affidavit disclosed that Yaroch had previously traveled abroad without reporting some of those trips to the FBI, despite security requirements.

His plans unraveled in July when he voluntarily disclosed his cryptocurrency thefts to Justice Department personnel.

The initial investigation identified roughly $1 million in suspected stolen cryptocurrency. However, the subsequent criminal case found that the scheme generated about $2.47 million.

The FBI terminated his employment on July 31, and investigators recovered approximately $925,426 in cryptocurrency from accounts he controlled that day. An additional $165,582 remained in dollars at Kraken, according to the affidavit.

Yaroch is scheduled to be sentenced on March 4, 2027, and faces up to 20 years in prison.

Thomas Tuchel reveals why Cole Palmer has been left out of England squad AGAIN

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Thomas Tuchel has explained Cole Palmer is still paying the price for missing too many England camps after the Chelsea star was once again left out of his Three Lions squad.

Palmer was left out of the World Cup squad in the summer and withdrew from the group for the extended international break in September and October due to injury.

Tuchel warned it was a ‘missed opportunity’ for Palmer at the time, insisting he ‘misses way too many camps’ with other players moving ahead of him in the pecking order as a result.

Palmer has played almost every minute of Chelsea’s Premier League campaign this term with his injury problems from last season seemingly behind him.

But Tuchel made the decision on Friday to leave him out of the squad for upcoming Nations League games against Croatia and Spain with Jobe Bellingham and Fulham’s Josh King named ahead of him.

Speaking on Friday, Tuchel explained King’s versatility was a key reason for his inclusion ahead of Palmer. Morgan Rogers and Morgan Gibbs-White also also vying for the no10 spot with Tuchel explaining the group that impressed him training last month keep their places on merit.

‘It is enough no10s [in the squad] and we stick with guys who were in camp because camp was excellent for the two-and-a-half weeks we had together,’ Tuchel said.

Tuchel added: ‘Everyone is desperate to play for England and Cole is no exception.

"It's a brave new world": GTA 6's PC port will release sooner than you think to avoid losing money to AI rip-offs, says former Rockstar tech director

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"If it's GTA, I think people are gonna try it."

GTA 6's PC release may come sooner than anticipated, according to former Rockstar North technical director Obbe Vermeij, because Rockstar and publishers Take-Two will be concerned about losing money to unofficial generative AI clones of the PS5 version, once it launches on 19th November. In general, Vermeij thinks that how to avoid having your games duplicated by chatbot herders is "the big question" right now for devs and publishers, particularly those who don't have the means to enforce their intellectual property dominions as vehemently as Take-Two do for Grand Theft Auto.

Speaking to me during a wide-ranging chatathon, Vermeij reflected on the early days of Grand Theft Auto development at Rockstar North in the 90s, and how the Noel-and-Liam-Gallagher-esque relationship between PC and console gaming has evolved. He shared a sweary anecdote about the company's difficulties getting the original GTA 1 to look the business on Sony's PlayStation.

"I didn't work on GTA 1 and 2, but I worked for the company at the time, and I was actually right next to the GTA 1 team, so I was able to follow it," he said. "So they worked on PC and then an external company in Dundee did the PlayStation version. Which was really weird, because like, PCs were so much more powerful - still the case, but in those days, it was even more so.

"And I remember, at the time, the GTA team was definitely the most rowdy of all of the teams," Vermeij continued. "And there was one particular day where they sent the PC build away, and then it came back from the PlayStation people, and they had cut down all the textures from 256 colours to 16 colours, and my god, the artist was just going around and swearing - 'what the fuck did they do to our textures, I can't believe it, we can't release this shite'. So it always has been that sort of awkward relationship between PC and the console. So in those days, it was like PC first and then console second, even though that didn't really make much sense for the sales, because the PlayStation versions sold way better."

When it came time to develop GTA 3, Rockstar "flipped that around", Vermeij went on, creating the console versions first. "Clearly the PC market has sort of grown, caught up with the console market, but there's still that same mindset," he said. Most publishers prefer to release all versions at once, Vermeij added, "because then there's only one marketing campaign they have to pay for." GTA lives by its own rules, however. "I guess it's less of an argument for them because the game is just so big, so they just kind of always left it that way. And for developers, it's way easier to just do one version at a time. So, you know, that makes sense."

The arrival of generative AI threatens to throw all this to the winds. Tools such as Reverse Engineer Anything allow for speedy unofficial ports or "slopcomps" of console games on PC, despite warnings from seasoned recomp developers that using genAI for this purpose wrecks the community aspect of unofficial porting, and results in illegible "bruteforce" code rather than preserving some understanding of how the original game was made.

"It really has changed the world," Vermeij observed, "and I think I would be concerned, if I was Rockstar, that somebody would just take the PS5 version and get AI to change it to PC, which won't be won't be as good as their effort, but it could still also hurt their sales. So, I wouldn't be surprised if there was an effort to get [the releases] a little bit closer together. Obviously, I don't really know what they're thinking."

GTA 6 is, according to Rockstar and Take-Two at least, an enormously fiddly production, costing hundreds of millions of dollars over eight years in development. Nonetheless, Vermeij thinks current genAI tools are "probably" capable of pulling off a port. "It will take a long time. It actually, physically takes hours and hours and hours to do these things. And it will take a bunch of tokens and everything, so it would probably cost like, tens of thousands of dollars in tokens. But like, you know, if it's GTA, I think people are gonna try it."

There are already signs that genAI PC port goldrushers are ready to pounce on GTA 6. Just today, Ars Technica reported on efforts to yassify the game's PS5 trailer footage by piping it through a stronker PC graphics card and using Nvidia's DLSS 5 to apply extra layers of gloss.

Vermeij himself left Rockstar way back in 2009 and is now an indie developer. His current early access project is Plentiful, a hexagonal god sim with an emphasis on water physics. It uses "AI-assisted machine translation for localization", but not for "gameplay content, art, or audio". Vermeij is, in general, interested in generative AI despite the surging tide of slopcomp ports. Smaller indie developers are, of course, more vulnerable to the financial fallout from having their work unofficially cloned by generative AI, but Vermeij thinks it's an existential worry for megacorps like Take-Two as well.

"I don't really see how you could protect against that, to be honest," he told me in closing. "I don't see it. But a big company like Take-Two, they would be able to get the lawyers on the case, and maybe, like, you said, for a big game, maybe the AI doesn't really do a particularly good job. But yeah, I mean, it's a brave new world. What was the case before, is that there's so much effort in developing the game, that it is not possible to rip it off, and that's completely changed now. So, yeah, we're going to have to see in the next couple of years, how that's going to pan out."

The latest rumour-mongering places GTA 6's PC release sometime in early 2027. In other news, Rockstar staff are soon to formally vote on whether to apply for union recognition. If the organisers carry the day, the resulting union could encompass nearly all the company's UK staff below management level.

All weapons and loot in Fortnite Treasure Hunters

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

One of the most exciting aspects of each new Fortnite season is the updated loot pool, which consistently brings fresh items to the island while also rotating back in old favorites. In Fortnite’s Treasure Hunters season, the loot pool has plenty to discover.

Discovering and keeping track of everything available to find around the island is essential, as certain items are sure to work better for your combat style than others. If you want to keep up with everything that’s up for grabs, here’s all of the weapons and loot you can find around the island in Fortnite Treasure Hunters.

The loot pool for Fortnite Treasure Hunters primarily features an array of One Piece and anime-themed items to complement the overall theme of the season. It also has plenty of unvaulted items from past seasons, though, so some previous favorite items of yours may have returned this time around.

As the season progresses, the existing loot pool will change. This includes both the addition of fresh items to use and the removal of others. Because of this, it’s a good idea to use any items you really like while you can, as there’s no guarantee they’ll stick around forever.

Later this season, a few additional unvaults have also been confirmed to arrive. This includes the Lever Action Shotgun, Frenzy Auto Shotgun, and Flapjack Rifle. A precise release date for them hasn’t been revealed yet.

Former Wolves, Sheffield United and Leicester City man retires aged 33 after ‘tough’ recent years

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Conor Coady was let go by Wrexham in August, with the club and player agreeing a mutual termination of his contract. The former Wolves captain has been without a club since, although there were links with a move to Swansea City shortly before his departure from the Racecourse Ground.

Now though, aged 33, Coady has announced his decision to retire from professional football. The defender confirmed the news on his Instagram, with a video thanking his former clubs, coaches, teammates, family and friends for their support along the way.

Coady hangs up his boots after over 500 club career appearances and 10 England caps. He played 317 times for Wolverhampton Wanderers and also spent time with Sheffield United, Huddersfield Town, Leicester City, Everton, Charlton Athletic, Wrexham and Liverpool.

In a video message shared on his Instagram, Coady bid a fond farewell to the game. He said:

“Football is my life. It always has been, but everything at some point comes to an end. I never ever thought that playing for Wolves in Europe or going to with England a World Cup this day would come. But honestly, the last couple of years have been tough – not playing as much as I would have liked I have struggled with, as I have lived for playing footy.

“Coming through at my boyhood club and putting the red shirt on was one of the proudest days, as no one could ever take that away from me. Sheffield United and Huddersfield were the perfect clubs for me and made me grow, then came Wolves, the place I needed and the place that made me a man.

“Eight of the best years, with the best people, in a city that made me their own. My kids were born in Old Gold, and you made Amie (Coady’s wife) truly enjoy football. For that, it will always be home.”

Coady would go on to highlight his pride at having played for Everton at Goodison Park, before reflecting on tougher times at Leicester City and Wrexham. He said:

“On to Leicester, then Wrexham, which were tough at times but filled with amazing people and amazing supporters who care deeply about their club. The Charlton, who put a smile back on my face.

“The truth is, I’m ready now. I’ve given football everything and I will continue to do so in my next chapter, but it’s time for my incredible wife Amie and my four boys to enjoy seeing Daddy that little bit more.”

After personal thanks to his close family, friends, supporters and long-serving agent James Lippett, Coady signed off by saying: “It’s been an honour to say I played this unbelievable game. Thank you for everything. Coads.”

Coady has cut a popular figure among fans and teammates alike across his playing career. It means there was no shortage of support for the retiring defender upon his announcement.

Wolves players of past and present sent their best wishes, including Kieran Trippier, Raul Jimenez, Romain Saiss, Daniel Podence and many more. Ex-Everton teammates Jack Grealish and James Tarkowski congratulated him too, alongside other prominent figures like former England boss Gareth Southgate, retired boxer Tony Bellew and broadcasters Kelly Somers and Suzi Perry.

Coady has worked in punditry in recent years, so it will be intriguing to see if he ventures further into this world after his retirement. If a future in coaching awaits, his personable character and leadership abilities would make him an excellent addition to a club’s academy or first-team staff.
